It's just making things difficult.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, do you feel like you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. Um, there were several nights recently where I only slept maybe three or four hours, but I still got up feeling... just really charged. Like coffee wasn't even necessary to get my day going. It's strange, but yeah, I guess I didn't feel like I needed the extra rest.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt charged on very little sleep, did you also feel unusually upbeat or euphoric?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. Um, earlier in the month I felt almost... lit up, I guess. Like I was just unstoppable. But it wasn't always a good feeling. It would kind of flip, and I'd get really short with people. Like, I’d snap at my mom, or even my supervisor at work, just over little questions that normally wouldn’t bother me at all.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt charged and lit up, were you more active than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Um, for a while there, I just had this huge burst of energy. I would get home from work and instead of relaxing, I was, like, deep-cleaning everything. I started making all these lists for home renovations, looking up interest rates, and even setting up ideas for side jobs. It was just... I was doing all of this after a full day of work, and it felt like I just couldn't shut it off.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, were you talking more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was. My sister actually pointed it out to me. She told me I was talking so fast about equity, taxes, and all those paint colors that she couldn’t even get a word in edgewise. And it's true, I could actually feel myself pushing to keep going. It was like I just had to get all the words out.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy times, did you have experiences that other people would find hard to believe, like feeling especially powerful or certain of things in a way that seemed unusual for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess you could say that. I just felt... really capable. I honestly started thinking I could pay my condo down super fast, pass all the CPA exams, and run a little bookkeeping business on the side. It just felt like failure wasn't an option. Like I could handle all of it without any problem.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did it cause serious problems at work, at home, or with other people?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I'm still getting to work and everything, so I haven't lost my job. But I guess it has caused some issues. I've made enough reconciliation mistakes lately that my supervisor actually pulled me aside to talk to me about it. And outside of work... I've just been avoiding my friends and skipping my regular salsa class. I just don't really feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period recently where you felt down or depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Um, lately I just wake up with this... this heavy, empty feeling. It's hard to explain, but even when I'm just sitting in my own condo—the one I worked so hard for—it just makes me want to cry. So, yeah, it's been like that for most of the day recently.\n\nDoctor: About how many days in a row did that high-energy, lit-up period last?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, honestly. I didn't keep track of the exact days. It just... it felt like a solid chunk of time where I was going non-stop, but I couldn't give you a specific number.\n\nDoctor: Did that high-energy period last at least 4 days in a row?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying, I really just can't say for sure. It might have been four days, or maybe it was only three. I just know it was a few days straight of feeling really wired, but I wasn't keeping track of the exact number, so I can't say for certain if it hit that four-day mark.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did you do anything unusually risky or impulsive for you, like spending more money than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't. Um, I didn't spend a bunch of extra money or do anything risky like that. I mean, I do bookkeeping for work, so I always keep a pretty close eye on my accounts anyway. I was just really energetic and getting a lot done, but I wasn't doing anything impulsive or out of control.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, were you hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn't. Um, I didn't go to the hospital or anything like that. I was just going about my normal routine, just with a lot more energy.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did other people clearly notice that you were acting unusually different from your normal self?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody said anything to me about it. I think I just seemed like I was having a really productive week at work. So, no, I don't think anyone noticed anything unusual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down like that for at least 2 weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely longer than two weeks. Um, this depressed, guilty fog has actually been hanging around for about five months now. It got a lot worse after I bought my condo, but yeah, it's been going on for a while.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
